\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\,f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}\mathbin{\cdot}\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{\pi_{0}}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\Phi\mathbin{\circ}\pi_{0}\right\rparen}-\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{\pi_{0}}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}f_{0}\mathbin{\circ}\pi_{0}\right\rparen}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\,f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}\mathbin{\cdot}\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\Phi^{\mathrm{v}}-\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}\qquad\text{for all }f_{0}\in\mathord{H_{0}^{1,\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\right\rparen}.

For
f=f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}\mathbin{\otimes}g_{0}^{\mathrm{h}}\in D_{1}
we get that

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}f

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\,f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}g_{0}^{\mathrm{h}}\mathbin{\cdot}\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\Phi^{\mathrm{v}}-g_{0}^{\mathrm{h}}\mathbin{\cdot}\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}+f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}\mathbin{\cdot}\frac{1}{\beta}\,\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{v}}}}\Phi^{\mathrm{h}}\right\rparen\left\lparen g_{0}^{\mathrm{h}}\right\rparen

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\,f\mathbin{\cdot}\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\Phi^{\mathrm{v}}-\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}f+\frac{1}{\beta}\,\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{v}}}}\Phi^{\mathrm{h}}\right\rparen(f).

In straight analogy to
[
GS14
, Section 4]
,
the proof of the next lemma,
which deals with the globally Lipschitzian case,
is based on a perturbation theorem for essentially m-dissipative operators.
We present it here for sake of completeness.

Theorem 3.14

(Kato perturbation of an essentially m-dissipative operator)
.

Let an essentially m-dissipative operator
Z

and a dissipative operator
T

have common domain in some given Hilbert space with norm

\lVert\cdot\rVert\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\sqrt{\left\lparen\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot\right\rparen}
.
Assume that there are constants
c_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
and
c_{2}\in(0,\infty)
such that

\lVert Tf\rVert^{2}\leq c_{1}\left\lparen Zf\mathbin{,}f\right\rparen+c_{2}\lVert f\rVert^{2}

holds for all
f
from the common domain.
Then, the perturbation
Z+T
of
Z
by
T
defined on the common domain
is essentially m-dissipative.

Proof.

See
[
Dav80
, Corollary 3.8, Lemma 3.9 and Problem 3.10]
.
∎

Lemma 3.15

(Essential m-dissipativity in case of globally Lipschitzian potentials)
.

Assume that
\Psi
is globally Lipschitzian.
Then,
(\widetilde{L},D_{1})
is essentially m-dissipative
on
\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\mathbin{\otimes_{\mathrm{loc}}}\nu\right\rparen}
.
Hence,
(L,D_{1})
is essentially m-dissipative
on the space
H=\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}}\mathbin{\otimes_{\mathrm{loc}}}\nu\right\rparen}
.

Proof.

Define
Z\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=L_{0}
on
D_{1}
.
Then,
(Z,D_{1})
is a dissipative extension of
(Z,D_{0})
.
Thus,
(Z,D_{1})
is essentially m-dissipative
on
\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\mathbin{\otimes_{\mathrm{loc}}}\nu\right\rparen}

by
Lemma
3.13
.
Define the perturbation

Tf\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=-\frac{1}{\beta}\,\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{v}}}}\Phi^{\mathrm{h}}\right\rparen(f)+\frac{1}{2}\,f\mathbin{\cdot}\,\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\Phi^{\mathrm{v}}

for all
f=f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}\mathbin{\otimes}g_{0}^{\mathrm{h}}\in D_{1}
.
Since by
2.39

(\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}},D_{1})
is antisymmetric
and also
(\widetilde{A},D_{1})
is antisymmetric
in
\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\mathbin{\otimes_{\mathrm{loc}}}\nu\right\rparen}
,

(T,D_{1})
is antisymmetric as well.
Thus,
(T,D_{1})
is dissipative.

Choose
g
such that
f=Ug
.
Using the Cauchy-Bunyakovsky-Schwarz inequality we get that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{v}}}}\Phi^{\mathrm{h}}(f)\right\rparen^{2}\ \mathrm{d}\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\mathbin{\otimes_{\mathrm{loc}}}\nu

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\lvert\mathord{\mathrm{v}\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{v}}}}\Phi^{\mathrm{h}}\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{v}}}}f\right\rparen}\rvert^{2}\ \mathrm{d}\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\mathbin{\otimes_{\mathrm{loc}}}\nu=\int_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\lvert\mathord{\mathrm{v}\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{v}}}}\Phi^{\mathrm{h}}\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{v}}}}g\right\rparen}\rvert^{2}\ \mathrm{d}\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}}\mathbin{\otimes_{\mathrm{loc}}}\nu

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{v}}}}\Phi^{\mathrm{h}}\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\to\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\mathbin{\otimes_{\mathrm{loc}}}\nu\right\rparen}}^{2}\mathbin{\cdot}\int_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\lvert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{v}}}}g\rvert_{\mathord{\mathrm{v}}}^{2}\ \mathrm{d}\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}}\mathbin{\otimes_{\mathrm{loc}}}\nu

\displaystyle=\lVert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\Phi\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\to\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mathord{\mathrm{m}}\right\rparen}}^{2}\mathbin{\cdot}\left\lparen-\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}}}}g\mathbin{,}g\right\rparen_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}}\mathbin{\otimes_{\mathrm{loc}}}\nu\right\rparen}}

\displaystyle=\lVert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\Phi\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\to\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mathord{\mathrm{m}}\right\rparen}}^{2}\mathbin{\cdot}\left\lparen-\widetilde{S}f\mathbin{,}f\right\rparen_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\mathbin{\otimes_{\mathrm{loc}}}\nu\right\rparen}}.

by the integration by parts formula (
2.1
).
Abbreviate

C_{\Phi}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\lVert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\Phi\rVert_{\mathord{L^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\to\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mathord{\mathrm{m}}\right\rparen}}^{2}
.
Then, we immediately conclude

\displaystyle\lVert Tf\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\mathbin{\otimes_{\mathrm{loc}}}\nu\right\rparen}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}\,C_{\Phi}\mathbin{\cdot}\left\lparen-\widetilde{S}f\mathbin{,}f\right\rparen_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\mathbin{\otimes_{\mathrm{loc}}}\nu\right\rparen}}+\frac{1}{4}\,C_{\Phi}\mathbin{\cdot}\lVert f\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\mathbin{\otimes_{\mathrm{loc}}}\nu\right\rparen}}^{2}

\displaystyle=c_{1}\left\lparen-Zf\mathbin{,}f\right\rparen_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\mathbin{\otimes_{\mathrm{loc}}}\nu\right\rparen}}+c_{2}\lVert f\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\mathbin{\otimes_{\mathrm{loc}}}\nu\right\rparen}}^{2}

with

c_{1}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\frac{2}{\alpha\beta}\,C_{\Phi}

and

c_{2}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\frac{1}{4}\,C_{\Phi}
, since we know

\left\lparen\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}f\mathbin{,}f\right\rparen_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\mathbin{\otimes_{\mathrm{loc}}}\nu\right\rparen}}=0
.
Finally, the claim follows applying
Theorem 3.14
to
(Z+T,D_{1})
.
∎

Corollary 3.16

(
(D2)
for globally Lipschitzian potentials)
.

Assume that
\Psi
is a globally Lipschitzian potential.
Then,
(L,D)
is essentially m-dissipative on
H
.

Proof.

Note that
(L,D)
is a dissipative extension of
(L,D_{0})
.
Thus, we show that
(L,D_{1})
is contained in the closure of
(L,D_{0})

and then apply
Lemma
3.15
.

Let
f=f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}\in\pi_{0}^{\ast}\mathord{H_{0}^{1,\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\right\rparen}
,

g\in\kappa^{\ast}\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\right\rparen}
and
a sequence
(f_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}}
in
\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\right\rparen}
such that
its vertical lifting approximates
f
in
\mathord{H^{1,2}}
-
sense, i. e.

(1)

f_{n}\longrightarrow f_{0}
as
n\to\infty

in
\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}};\mathord{\mathrm{m}}\right\rparen}
-
sense and

(2)

\frac{\partial f_{n}}{\partial x^{j}}\longrightarrow\frac{\partial f_{0}}{\partial x^{j}}
as
n\to\infty

in
\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}};\mathord{\mathrm{m}}\right\rparen}
-
sense
for any chart
x=(x^{j})_{j=1}^{\text{n}}
.

This convergence is maintained under passing to

\pi_{0}^{\ast}\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}\right\rparen}
, i. e. weighting the manifold.
Finally, we conclude that

L\left\lparen f_{n}^{\mathrm{v}}\mathbin{\otimes}g\right\rparen\longrightarrow L\left\lparen f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}\mathbin{\otimes}g\right\rparen=L\left\lparen f\mathbin{\otimes}g\right\rparen\qquad\text{in }H\text{ as }n\to\infty.

∎

The final prove of this section basically is the same as in
[
GS14
, Theorem 4.7]

as we have taken geometric effects into account before.

Theorem 3.17

(
(D2)
for locally Lipschitzian potentials)
.

Let
\Psi
be a loc-Lipschitzian potential bounded from below.
Then,
(L,D)
is essentially m-dissipative on
H
.

Proof.

Wlog. we assume that
\Psi\geq 0
.
Let
\varepsilon\in(0,\infty)
and fix some
g\in D\setminus\{0\}
.
Choose
\varphi,\psi\in D
such that

\varphi|_{\supp(g)}=1\text{,}\quad\psi|_{\supp(\varphi)}=1\quad\text{and}\quad 0\leq\varphi\leq\psi\leq 1.

Let
f\in D
arbitrary.
Throughout the proof,
we add to the generators and invariant measures a subscript to indicate the corresponding potential,
e. g.
\mu_{0}=\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\mathrm{s}}}}
in case of the zero potential.
By construction
and using dissipativity of
(L_{\psi\Psi},D)
on
\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mu_{\psi\Psi}\right\rparen}

we get that

\displaystyle\lVert(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}-L_{\Psi})(\varphi f)-g\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mu_{\Psi}\right\rparen}}

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\varphi\left\lparen(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}-L_{\psi\Psi})f-g\right\rparen\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mu_{\psi\Psi}\right\rparen}}+\lVert f\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mu_{\psi\Psi}\right\rparen}}\mathbin{\cdot}\lVert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\varphi\rVert_{\mathord{L^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}}\right\rparen}}

\displaystyle\leq\lVert(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}-L_{\psi\Psi})f-g\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mu_{\psi\Psi}\right\rparen}}+\lVert(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}-L_{\psi\Psi})f\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mu_{\psi\Psi}\right\rparen}}\mathbin{\cdot}\lVert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\varphi\rVert_{\mathord{L^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}}\right\rparen}}.

Now, we tighten the requirements on
\varphi
via additionally demanding that

\lVert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\varphi\rVert_{\mathord{L^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\to\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}\right\rparen}}=\lVert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\varphi\rVert_{\mathord{L^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}\right\rparen}}<\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\mathbin{\cdot}\lVert g\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mu_{0}\right\rparen}}^{-1}=\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\mathbin{\cdot}\lVert g\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{s}}\right\rparen}}^{-1}.

Due to
Corollary
3.16

(L_{\psi\Psi},D)
is essentially m-dissipative on
\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mu_{\psi\Psi}\right\rparen}
,
hence as a consequence of the Lumer-Philips Theorem,
there is
f\in D
such that simultaneously hold

\displaystyle\lVert(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}-L_{\psi\Psi})f-g\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mu_{\psi\Psi}\right\rparen}}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\lVert(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}-L_{\psi\Psi})f\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mu_{\psi\Psi}\right\rparen}}\leq 2\lVert g\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mu_{\psi\Psi}\right\rparen}}.

For such an
f
we end up with

\lVert(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}-L_{\Psi})(\varphi f)-g\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mu_{\psi\Psi}\right\rparen}}<\varepsilon
.
In conclusion, we proved that

(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}-L_{\Psi})(D)
is dense in
H=\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mu_{\Psi}\right\rparen}
.
∎

3.2.
Hypocoercivity conditions

Lemma 3.18

(algebraic relation
(H1)
)
.

Let condition
(P1)
hold.
Then, we have
PAP|_{D}=0
.

Proof.

Recall
Equation (
3.6
)

from
Remark
3.9
.
Furthermore, we are going to apply the formula for Gaussian integrals from
[
GS14
, Lemma 3.1]
:
Let
f\in D

and consider polar coordinates in the fibre at
x\in\mathord{\mathbb{M}}

using
[
For12
, Satz 14.8]

which is an application of the transformation formula and Fubini.
We get that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}APf\ \mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle=\int_{(0,\infty)}\int_{\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}}}-\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{x}\left\lparen v(r,u)\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f](x)\right\rparen}\mathbin{\cdot}r^{\text{n}-1}\,\frac{d\nu(v(r,u))}{d\mathord{\lambda}\otimes\mathord{\mathrm{S}}}\quad\mathrm{d}\mathord{\mathrm{S}}(u)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\int_{(0,\infty)}0\mathbin{\cdot}r^{\text{n}-1}\,\frac{d\nu(v(r,u_{0}))}{d\mathord{\lambda}\otimes\mathord{\mathrm{S}}}\ \mathrm{d}r=0,

where
u_{0}\in\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}}
is arbitrary,
and further
\mathord{\mathrm{S}}
denotes the surface measure of the sphere
\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}}
and

v\colon\ (0,\infty)\times\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}}\to v((0,\infty)\times\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}})\subseteq\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}},\ (r,u)\longmapsto v(r,u)

is the diffeomorphism corresponding to

(0,\infty)\times\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}}\to F=\mathbb{R}^{\text{n}},\,(r,u)\mapsto ru
.
We point out that
this argument just works
as
\nu
is invariant wrt. rotations.

We have seen that
P_{S}AP
is trivial an
D
,
so we can use orthogonality of
P_{S}
to obtain

0=\left\lparen P_{S}APf\mathbin{,}1\right\rparen_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}\right\rparen}}=\left\lparen APf\mathbin{,}1\right\rparen_{H}\qquad\text{for all }f\in D.

Thus,
PAP|_{D}=0
.
∎

Lemma 3.19

(microscopic hypocoercivity
(H2)
)
.

Let condition
(P1)
hold.

Then, condition
(H2)
is fulfilled with

\Lambda_{m}=\alpha
.

Proof.

Let
f\in D
.
Using the Poincaré inequality for Gaussian measures, see
[
Bec89
]
,
we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\lparen-Sf\mathbin{,}f\right\rparen_{H}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{\beta}\,\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}}}}f\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}}}}f\right\rparen_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\to\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{s}}}\right\rparen}}=\frac{\alpha}{\beta}\,\lVert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{v}}}}f\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\to\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{s}}}\right\rparen}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\alpha\lVert f-\left\lparen f\mathbin{,}1\right\rparen_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}}\right\rparen}}\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{s}}}\right\rparen}}^{2}=\alpha\lVert(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{H}-P_{S})f\rVert_{H}^{2}

and the claim follows.
∎

The strategy for proving condition
(H3)
relies on
[
GS14
, Corollary 2.13]
.
Most importantly, we have to prove that

(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{H}-PA^{2}P,D)
is essentially m-dissipative.
To do so, we characterise
\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{H}-PA^{2}P
on
D

starting from
Equation (
3.6
)

and show that the range
(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{H}-PA^{2}P)(D)
is dense in
H
.

Let
s\colon\,(-\delta,\delta)\to\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
be a curve such that

s(0)=v
and
s^{\prime}(0)=\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}(v)

for
v\in\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
fixed and some small
\delta\in(0,\infty)
.
Let
x\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\pi_{0}(v)
.
The following computation relies on

\pi_{0}\mathbin{\circ}s
being a geodesic of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}

and the characterisation of the directional derivative
in terms of parallel transport ‘
\paralleltransport
’ along
s
given by the Levi-Civita connection:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} -\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}APf(v)&\overset{\eqref{eq:Langevin:APf-Eq2}}{=}-\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\left\lparen-\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{\pi_{0}}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}\left[f\right]\mathbin{\circ}\pi_{0}\right\rparen}\right\rparen(v)\\
&=\mathord{\mathrm{s}_{v}\left\lparen\lim_{t\to 0}\frac{1}{t}\,\left\lparen\paralleltransport_{s(t)}^{s(t)}(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathbin{\circ}s(t))-\paralleltransport_{s(0)}^{s(t)}(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathbin{\circ}s(0))\right\rparen\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{h}}}}P_{S}f(v)\right\rparen}\\
&\qquad\qquad+\mathord{\mathrm{h}_{v}\left\lparen\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}(v)\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}^{\mathord{\mathrm{s}}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{h}}}}P_{S}f\right\rparen(v)\right\rparen}\\
&=\mathord{\mathrm{h}_{v}\left\lparen\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}(v)\mathbin{,}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}\left[\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{h}}}}f\right](v)\right\rparen}+\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{x}\left\lparen v\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{v}^{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\right\rparen(x)\right\rparen}\\
&=\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{x}\left\lparen v\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}\left[f\right](x)\right\rparen}+\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{x}\left\lparen v\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{v}^{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\right\rparen(x)\right\rparen}.\end{aligned}

For the second equality we also used the metric compatibility of the Levi-Civita connection
\mathord{\nabla^{\mathord{\mathrm{s}}}}
.
The last but one line is obtained
using
Lemma
A.2

for the second summand.
Now, we transform into polar coordinates in the fibre
\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}

similar as in the proof of
Lemma
3.18
.
With this ansatz we calculate
applying
[
GS14
, Lemma 3.1]
twice that

(3.8)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}-\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\left\lparen-\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{\pi_{0}}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}\left[f\right]\mathbin{\circ}\pi_{0}\right\rparen}\right\rparen\ \mathrm{d}\nu\\
={}&\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{x}\left\lparen v\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}\left[f\right](x)\right\rparen}\ \mathrm{d}\nu(v)+\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{x}\left\lparen v\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{v}^{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\right\rparen(x)\right\rparen}\ \mathrm{d}\nu(v)\\
={}&\int_{(0,\infty)}\int_{\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}}}\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{x}\left\lparen v(r,u)\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{v(r,u)}^{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\right\rparen(x)\right\rparen}\\
&\hphantom{\int_{(0,\infty)}\int_{\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}}}}\qquad\qquad\mathbin{\cdot}r^{\text{n}-1}\,\frac{d\nu(v(r,u))}{d\mathord{\lambda}\otimes\mathord{\mathrm{S}}}\quad\mathrm{d}\mathord{\mathrm{S}}(u)\,\mathrm{d}r\\
={}&\frac{1}{\text{n}}\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f](x)\quad\mathbin{\cdot}\int_{(0,\infty)}r^{2}\mathbin{\cdot}\left\lparen\frac{1}{\mathord{\mathrm{S}}(\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}})}\,\int_{\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}}}1\ \mathrm{d}\mathord{\mathrm{S}}\right\rparen\mathbin{\cdot}r^{\text{n}-1}\,\frac{d\nu(v(r,u_{0}))}{d\mathord{\lambda}\otimes\mathord{\mathrm{S}}}\ \mathrm{d}r\\
={}&\frac{1}{\beta}\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f](x)\quad\mathbin{\cdot}1,\end{aligned}

where we have taken
u_{0}\in\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}}
arbitrary,
since
\nu
is invariant wrt. rotations.
In order to arrive at the last but one line,
we consider some chart
\left\lparen x^{j}\right\rparen_{j=1}^{\text{n}}
at
x\in\mathord{\mathbb{M}}
providing normal coordinates;
in such coordinates the Levi-Civita connection is understood in terms of directional derivatives as

\mathord{\nabla_{\mathcal{Y}}}\mathcal{X}(x)=\sum_{i,j\in\{1,\ldots,\text{n}\}}\mathcal{Y}^{i}(x)\,\frac{\partial\mathcal{X}^{j}(x)}{\partial x^{i}}\,x^{j}

for all
\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}\in\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\textstyle\Gamma^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}{\scriptstyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma^{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\right\rparen}
with local coordinate expression

\mathcal{X}=\sum_{j=1}^{\text{n}}\mathcal{X}^{j}\mathbin{\cdot}\partial x^{j}

and

\mathcal{Y}=\sum_{j=1}^{\text{n}}\mathcal{Y}^{j}\mathbin{\cdot}\partial x^{j}
.
Thus, we can understand the mapping

\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}}\to\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}},\ u\longmapsto\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{u}^{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\right\rparen(x)

as the matrix in
[
GS14
, Lemma 3.1]
.
The last step of
Equation (
3.8
)
is due to the fact that
the mean of a chi-squared distribution equals the number of degrees of freedom, i. e.
n
in the present case.

With the proof of part
(ii)
of
Lemma
3.4

and with
[
GS14
, Lemma 3.1]

we similarly get that
for every
v\in\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
with
x\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\pi_{0}(v)
holds

(3.9)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &P_{S}\left\lparen\frac{1}{\beta}\,\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{v}}}}\Phi^{\mathrm{h}}(APf)\right\rparen(v)=\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\Phi^{\mathrm{v}}\mathbin{\cdot}APf\ \mathrm{d}\nu\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:Langevin:APf-Eq2}}{=}{}&-\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathord{\mathrm{h}\left\lparen\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{h}}}}\Phi^{\mathrm{v}}\right\rparen}\mathbin{\cdot}\mathord{\mathrm{h}\left\lparen\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{h}}}}(P_{S}f)\right\rparen}\ \mathrm{d}\nu\\
={}&-\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{x}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\Phi(x)\right\rparen}\mathbin{\cdot}\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{x}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f](x)\right\rparen}\ \mathrm{d}\nu\\
={}&-\frac{1}{\beta}\,\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{x}\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\Phi(x)\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f](x)\right\rparen}=-\frac{1}{\beta}\,\mathord{\nabla_{\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\Phi}}(\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f])(x).\end{aligned}

Hence, we proved combining
Equation (
3.8
)

and
Equation (
3.9
)
that

(3.10)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} PA^{2}Pf&=P_{S}A^{2}Pf=\frac{1}{\beta}\mathbin{\cdot}\left\lparen\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\mathbin{\circ}\pi_{0}-\mathord{\nabla_{\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\Psi}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\mathbin{\circ}\pi_{0}\right\rparen\\
&=\frac{1}{\beta}\mathbin{\cdot}\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}}}}\left\lparen\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]^{\mathrm{v}}\right\rparen=\frac{1}{\beta}\mathbin{\cdot}\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}}}}(P_{S}f)\end{aligned}

for all
f\in D
.
Compare our result to
[
GS16
, Equation (3.16)]
.
These preparations give shape to the following corollary,
cf.
[
GS16
, Proposition 3.9]
.

Corollary 3.20

(
PA^{2}P
is essentially m-dissipative)
.

Let condition
(P1)
hold.
Then, the range
(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{H}-PA^{2}P)(D)
is dense in
H
,
thus
PA^{2}P
is essentially m-dissipative on
D
.

Proof.

Right away, we know that

(PA^{2}P,D)\overset{\eqref{eq:Langevin:PA2P}}{=}\left\lparen\nicefrac{{1}}{{\beta}}\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}}}}\mathbin{\circ}P_{S},D)\right\rparen

is essentially m-dissipative on

P_{S}(H)=\pi_{0}^{\ast}\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}\right\rparen}
,
as
P_{S}(D)=\pi_{0}^{\ast}\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\right\rparen}

and
(\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}}}},D)

is essentially self-adjoint in
H
.
The later is true,
since
(\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}}}},D_{0})
is essentially self-adjoint in
H
–
as so is the Laplace-Beltrami on
(\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}},\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}})
,
cf. the beginning of
Section 2
–
and the fact that
D_{0}
is dense in
D
.

Let
g\in H
such that

\left\lparen(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{H}-PA^{2}P)f\mathbin{,}g\right\rparen_{H}=0

for all
f\in D

and we claim that
g=0
.
Our assumption immediately implies that

0=\left\lparen(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{H}-PA^{2}P)f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}\mathbin{,}P_{S}g\right\rparen_{H}=\left\lparen f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}-\frac{1}{\beta}\,\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}}}}f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}\mathbin{,}P_{S}g\right\rparen_{\pi_{0}^{\ast}\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}\right\rparen}}

for all
f_{0}\in\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\right\rparen}
.
Thus,
P_{S}g=0
,
since the range

\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{H}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{\beta}}\,\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}}}}\right\rparen(\pi_{0}^{\ast}\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\right\rparen})
is dense in

\pi_{0}^{\ast}\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}\right\rparen}
.
Ultimately, this means that

\left\lparen f\mathbin{,}g\right\rparen_{H}=\left\lparen PA^{2}Pf\mathbin{,}g\right\rparen_{H}=\left\lparen\nicefrac{{1}}{{\beta}}\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}}}}(P_{S}f)\mathbin{,}P_{S}g\right\rparen_{\pi_{0}^{\ast}\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}\right\rparen}}=0\qquad\text{for all }f\in D,

which implies

\left\lparen f\mathbin{,}g\right\rparen_{H}=0

for all
f\in D
,
hence
g=0
as claimed.
∎

Proposition 3.21

(macroscopic hypocoercivity
(H3)
)
.

Let the conditions
(P1)
and
(P2)
hold.
Then, condition
(H3)
is fulfilled
with
\Lambda_{M}=\frac{1}{\beta}\,\Lambda
.

Proof.

Let
f\in D
.
Since

\left\lparen PA^{2}P,D\right\rparen=\left\lparen\nicefrac{{1}}{{\beta}}\,\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}}}}P_{S},D\right\rparen

pregenerates a variant of the weighted horizontal gradient form in the sense that

\left\lparen PA^{2}Pf\mathbin{,}g\right\rparen_{H}=-\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathord{\mathrm{h}\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{h}}}}P_{S}f\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{h}}}}P_{S}g\right\rparen}\ \mathrm{d}\mu\qquad\text{for all }f,g\in D,

we easily compute that

\displaystyle\lVert APf\rVert_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\lvert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{h}}}}P_{S}f(v)\rvert_{\mathord{\mathrm{h}}}^{2}\quad\mathrm{d}\mu(v)=\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\lvert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f](x)\rvert_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}^{2}\quad\mathrm{d}\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\beta}\,\lVert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\to\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}\right\rparen}}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{\beta}\,\Lambda\lVert\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]-\left\lparen\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\mathbin{,}1\right\rparen_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}\right\rparen}}\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}\right\rparen}}^{2}

by Poincaré inequality.
Combining this estimates with the previous corollary,
then
[
GS14
, Corollary 2.13]
finishes the proof.
∎

The remaining hypocoercivity condition
(H4)
is checked
via a standard procedure relying on
[
GS14
, Lemma 2.14]
and
[
GS14
, Proposition 2.15]

cf. also
[
GS16
, Proposition 3.11]
.

Lemma 3.22

(boundedness of
(BS,D)
, first part of
(H4)
)
.

Let condition
(P1)
hold.
Then, with
c_{1}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\frac{1}{2}\,\alpha
it holds that

\lVert BSf\rVert_{H}\leq c_{1}\lVert(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{H}-P_{j})f\rVert_{H}\qquad\text{for all }f\in D

and
P_{j}\in\{P,P_{S}\}
,
j\in\{1,2\}
.

Proof.

First, we show that

SAP=\alpha AP

on
D
.
For the first time, it will become important here that

\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}
is not just a semispray,
but actually a
spray
, i. e. additionally we have that

\mathord{\left[\mathcal{V}\mathbin{,}\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\right]}=\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}
.
This is due to the fact that

\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}
was chosen in correspondence to the Levi-Civita connection
which is an affine connection.
See
[
APS60
]
.
Let
f\in D
be fixed.
Then, we immediately get that

SAPf=SAP_{S}f=-\alpha\mathcal{V}(-\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}(P_{S}f))=\alpha\mathcal{V}\left\lparen\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\left\lparen\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\right\rparen^{\mathrm{v}}\right\rparen,

since we know from the proof of
Lemma
3.4

that
\left\lparen Ah\mathbin{,}1\right\rparen_{H}=0
for all
h\in D
.
Using the Koszul formula we calculate that
for all
\mathcal{X}\in\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\textstyle\Gamma^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}{\scriptstyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma^{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\right\rparen}
holds

\mathcal{V}\mathord{\mathrm{h}\left\lparen\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\mathbin{,}\horlift\mathcal{X}\right\rparen}=0-\mathord{\mathrm{h}\left\lparen\mathord{\left[\mathcal{V}\mathbin{,}\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\right]}\mathbin{,}\horlift\mathcal{X}\right\rparen}+\mathord{\mathrm{h}\left\lparen\mathord{\left[\mathcal{V}\mathbin{,}\horlift\mathcal{X}\right]}\mathbin{,}\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\right\rparen}.

Similar to
[
GK02
, Proposition 5.1]

mentioned before in
Lemma
3.11
,
one could use local coordinates for
\mathcal{V}
in order to show that

\mathord{\left[\mathcal{V}\mathbin{,}\horlift\mathcal{X}\right]}

is purely vertical.
As
\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}
even is a spray,
we gain that

\displaystyle SAPf

\displaystyle=\alpha\mathcal{V}\mathord{\mathrm{h}\left\lparen\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{h}}}}\left\lparen\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\right\rparen^{\mathrm{v}}\right\rparen}=-\alpha\mathord{\mathrm{h}\left\lparen\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{h}}}}\left\lparen\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\right\rparen^{\mathrm{v}}\right\rparen}

\displaystyle=-\alpha\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\left\lparen\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\right\rparen^{\mathrm{v}}=\alpha(-\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}\left\lparen\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\right\rparen^{\mathrm{v}})=\alpha APf

Setting
c_{1}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\frac{\alpha}{2}

the claim follows with
[
GS14
, Lemma 2.14]
.
∎

Lemma 3.23

(boundedness of
(BA(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{H}-P),D)
, second part of
(H4)
))
.

Let the potential conditions
3.1
hold.
Then, there exists a constant
c_{2}\in(0,\infty)
such that

\lVert BA(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{H}-P)f\rVert_{H}\leq c_{2}\lVert(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{H}-P_{j})f\rVert_{H}\qquad\text{for all }f\in D

and
P_{j}\in\{P,P_{S}\}
,
j\in\{1,2\}
.

Proof.

Let
f\in D
and
g\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{H}-PA^{2}P)f
.
We know that

g\in D(\mathchoice{\displaystyle(BA)^{\ast}}{\textstyle(BA)^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\ast$}}}{\scriptstyle(BA)^{\ast}}{\scriptscriptstyle(BA)^{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\ast$}}})

with
\mathchoice{\displaystyle(BA)^{\ast}}{\textstyle(BA)^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\ast$}}}{\scriptstyle(BA)^{\ast}}{\scriptscriptstyle(BA)^{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\ast$}}}g=-A^{2}Pf
,
cf.
[
GS14
, Proposition 2.15]
.
Using our knowledge from the proof of part
(ii)
of
Lemma
3.4
,
furthermore
Equation (
3.6
)

and the Cauchy-Bunyakovsky-Schwarz inequality
we estimate that

\displaystyle\lVert\mathchoice{\displaystyle(BA)^{\ast}}{\textstyle(BA)^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\ast$}}}{\scriptstyle(BA)^{\ast}}{\scriptscriptstyle(BA)^{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\ast$}}}g\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\leq\lVert v\longmapsto\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}^{2}\left\lparen\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]^{\mathrm{v}}\right\rparen(v)\rVert_{H}+\left\lVert v\longmapsto\frac{1}{\beta}\,\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{v}}}}\Phi^{\mathrm{h}}(APf)(v)\right\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\lvert\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}^{2}\left\lparen\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]^{\mathrm{v}}\right\rparen\rvert\rVert_{H}+\lVert\lvert\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\Phi^{\mathrm{v}}\rvert_{\mathord{\mathrm{h}}}\mathbin{\cdot}\lvert\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}(\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]^{\mathrm{v}})\rvert_{\mathord{\mathrm{h}}}\rVert_{H}

\displaystyle\leq\lVert v\mapsto\lvert v\rvert_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}^{2}\rVert_{H}\mathbin{\cdot}\left\lparen\lVert\lvert\operatorname{Hess}_{\mathord{\mathrm{s}}}\left\lparen\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]^{\mathrm{v}}\right\rparen\rvert\rVert_{H}+\lVert\lvert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{h}}}}\Phi^{\mathrm{v}}\rvert_{\mathord{\mathrm{h}}}\mathbin{\cdot}\lvert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{h}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]^{\mathrm{v}}\rvert_{\mathord{\mathrm{h}}}\rVert_{H}\right\rparen

\displaystyle=\frac{\text{n}}{\beta}\left\lparen\lVert\lvert\operatorname{Hess}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\left\lparen\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\right\rparen\rvert^{\mathrm{v}}\rVert_{H}+\lVert\lvert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\Phi\rvert_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}^{\mathrm{v}}\mathbin{\cdot}\lvert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\rvert_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}^{\mathrm{v}}\rVert_{H}\right\rparen.

Due to the form of
PA^{2}P
we derived in
Equation (
3.10
)
,
we know that
u\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=Pf
solves the elliptic equation

\displaystyle u-\frac{1}{\beta}\,\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}}}}P_{S}u=g

in

\displaystyle\left\{u\in\pi_{0}^{\ast}\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}\right\rparen}\ \middle|\ \exists\,f_{0}\in\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\right\rparen}\colon\ u=f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}-\left\lparen f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}\mathbin{,}1\right\rparen_{H}\right\}.

As we assumed the necessary potential conditions,
the a priori estimates of Dolbeault, Mouhot and Schmeiser,
cf.
[
GS14
, Appendix]
,
yield existence of a constant
c_{2}\in(0,\infty)
independent of
Pf
and
g

such that

\lVert\mathchoice{\displaystyle(BA)^{\ast}}{\textstyle(BA)^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\ast$}}}{\scriptstyle(BA)^{\ast}}{\scriptscriptstyle(BA)^{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\ast$}}}g\rVert_{H}\leq c_{2}\mathbin{\cdot}\lVert Pg\rVert_{H}\leq c_{2}\mathbin{\cdot}\lVert g\rVert_{H}.

Now,
[
GS14
, Propositions 2.15]
does apply which finishes the proof.
∎

Collecting the individual results of
Section 3.1
and
Section 3.2

we can infer
Theorem
3.2

using the
2.2
.

4.
Hypocoercivity for the fibre lay-down model

In this section we demonstrate that the hypocoercivity method does apply
to the fibre lay-down model with the position space being a Riemannian manifold.
In other words, we generalise the results from
[
GS14
]

to the case of higher-order SDAEs with abstract position manifolds.
The statement, which is an application of the
2.2
, reads as follows:

Theorem 4.1

(Hypocoercivity of the geometric fibre lay-down dynamic)
.

Let
\sigma\in(0,\infty)

and
(\mathord{\mathbb{M}},\mathord{\mathrm{m}})
be a Riemannian manifold satisfying
2.1
.
Furthermore, let the potential conditions
3.1
hold.
We assume that

\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}|_{\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\Psi^{\mathrm{v}}=(\text{n}-1)\,\Psi^{\mathrm{h}}

holds.
Denote by
\nu
the normalised surface measure on
(\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}},\mathop{\mathfrak{B}(\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}})})

and define

\mu\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}}\mathbin{\otimes_{\mathrm{loc}}}\nu
.

Then, the fibre lay-down operator

\left\lparen L,\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\right\rparen}\right\rparen=\left\lparen\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\,\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}+\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}-\frac{1}{\text{n}-1}\,\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\Psi^{\mathrm{v}}),\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\right\rparen}\right\rparen

is closable in
H\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}};\mu\right\rparen}
.
Moreover, its closure
\mathord{\left\lparen L,\mathord{D\left\lparen L\right\rparen}\right\rparen}
generates a strongly continuous contraction semigroup
(T_{t})_{t\in[0,\infty)}
.
Finally, there are constants
\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in(0,\infty)

computable in terms of
\text{n}=\dim(\mathord{\mathbb{M}})
,
\Lambda
,
c
and
\sigma

such that for all
g\in H
and times
t\in[0,\infty)
holds

\lVert T_{t}g-\left\lparen g\mathbin{,}1\right\rparen_{H}\rVert_{H}\leq\kappa_{1}\mathord{\mathrm{e}}^{-\kappa_{2}t}\lVert g-\left\lparen g\mathbin{,}1\right\rparen_{H}\rVert_{H}.

Langevin-type models serve as surrogate models for fibre dynamics under a turbulent regime,
since the model including all physical details,
see
[
MW07
]
,
leads to enormous computational effort.
In view of the production process,
it’s a reasonable model assumption that
velocities should be normalised,
as fibre filaments are extruded with constant speed,
see e. g.
[
KMW12
,
GS13
,
Sti14
]
and various references therein.
Hence, in contrast to
Section 3

we look at an SDAE on
\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
incorporating the ‘polynomial-type’ normalisation assumption on the velocities.
In distinction from fibre lay-down applications with a different focus like in
[
LMS+17
]
,
we subsume possible side conditions on the position variables just in the position manifold
\mathord{\mathbb{M}}
.
Whenever we speak of an SDAE in this paper,
there is no ambiguity wrt. the nature of the algebraic side condition.

We implement the algebraic side condition geometrically
via replacing the standard fibre
F=\mathbb{R}^{\text{n}}
by
F=\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}}
,
thus the tangent bundle over
\mathord{\mathbb{M}}
by the unit tangent bundle

\mathord{\pi_{0|\mathrm{S}}}\colon\,\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\mathrel{\longrightarrow}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}
.
This modification has several side effects,
as the unit tangent bundle is a submanifold

\mathsf{Q}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\leq\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}

and consequently we consider the SDAE as an SDE in the sub
-
fibre bundle

\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\leq\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
.
Our choice of the Sasaki metric on
\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
is important here,
as the normal bundle of
\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
,
which is just the quotient bundle

\nicefrac{{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}|_{\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}}}{{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}}
in the first place,
can be realised as the orthogonal complement

\mathord{\mathrm{N}\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}^{\perp}

of
\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
wrt. the chosen metric.
This gives us the normal bundle really as a sub
-
fibre bundle

\mathord{\mathrm{N}\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\leq\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}

and the Whitney sum

\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}|_{\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}=\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\oplus\mathord{\mathrm{N}\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
.
It should not be too surprising at this point that
we can just restrict the horizontal bundle as

\mathord{\mathrm{H}\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\mathord{\mathrm{H}\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}|_{\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}
,
but are forced to replace the vertical lift by another lifting procedure.
We think that the problem has been explained very well in
[
FyE01
, Abschnitt 2.1]
:
Let’s consider
v\in\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
and
a\in\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{v}}{\textstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{v}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v$}}}\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
.
Then, there exists a
w\in\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{\pi_{0}(v)}}{\textstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\pi_{0}(v)$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{\pi_{0}(v)}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\pi_{0}(v)$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
such that
w
is vertically lifted to
a
at
v
,
i. e.
\vertlift_{v}(w)=a
.
However, we do not find a vector field
\mathcal{X}\in\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\textstyle\Gamma^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}{\scriptstyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma^{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\right\rparen}

– not even a local vector field –
such that

\vertlift_{v}(\mathcal{X})=a

and

\vertlift(\mathcal{X})\bigr|_{\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\in\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\textstyle\Gamma^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}{\scriptstyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma^{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\right\rparen}

hold.
In other words, the vertical lift of a vector field does not need to be tangent to the unit tangent bundle.
Thus, we adapt the vertical lift slightly in order to guarantee the lift of elements in
\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{\pi_{0}(v)}}{\textstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\pi_{0}(v)$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{\pi_{0}(v)}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\pi_{0}(v)$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
are elements of
\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{v}}{\textstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{v}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v$}}}\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}
.
For the next definition see also
[
BVA97
,
BV01
]
.

Definition 4.2

(tangential lift)
.

Let
x\in\mathord{\mathbb{M}}
,
v\in\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
and
u\in\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
.
The
tangent lift of
v
is defined as

\tanlift_{u}(v)\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\vertlift_{u}\left(v-\mathord{\mathrm{m}\left\lparen v\mathbin{,}u\right\rparen}\mathbin{\cdot}u\right)=\vertlift_{u}(v)-\mathord{\mathrm{m}\left\lparen v\mathbin{,}u\right\rparen}\mathbin{\cdot}\mathcal{N}_{\mathord{\mathrm{s}}}(u),

where the unit normal vector field

\mathcal{N}_{\mathord{\mathrm{s}}}\in\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\textstyle\Gamma^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}{\scriptstyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma^{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{N}\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\right\rparen}
has the following properties:

\mathord{\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{\mathord{\mathrm{s}}}\mathbin{,}d\pi_{0}\right\rangle}=0\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathord{\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{\mathord{\mathrm{s}}}\mathbin{,}d\kappa\right\rangle}=\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}.

We call
\mathcal{N}_{\mathord{\mathrm{s}}}
the
Sasakian normal vector field
,
since this normal vector field depends on our choice of the Sasaki metric on
\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
as outlined above.
∎

The tangential lift enables us to write

\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}=\tanlift(\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}})\oplus\mathord{\mathrm{H}\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}
as an Ehresmann connection
and furthermore the decomposition

\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}|_{\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}=\tanlift(\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}})\oplus\mathord{\mathrm{H}\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\oplus\mathord{\mathrm{N}\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}.

That said, starting from
Section 4.1

we might write
f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}

for given
f_{0}\in\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\right\rparen}
and
from the context it should be clear that we mean

\mathord{\pi_{0|\mathrm{S}}}^{\ast}f_{0}=f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}|_{\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\in\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\right\rparen}
.
Similarly, we just write
\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}

instead of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}|_{\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}
et cetera.
Be that as it may,
one should pay attention to read e. g.
\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{h}}}}f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}=\horlift|_{\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}(\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}f_{0})

with
\horlift|_{\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}

being the restriction of the horizontal lift to
\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
.
Moreover, the horizontal lift of
f_{0}
is up to constants characterised by

\mathord{\left\langle a\mathbin{,}df_{0}^{\mathrm{h}}\right\rangle}=\mathord{\left\langle a\mathbin{,}df_{0}\mathbin{\circ}d\kappa\right\rangle}

for all
a\in\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
,
and one may think it as

f_{0}^{\mathrm{h}}(v)=\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{v}}{\textstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{v}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v$}}}\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}df_{0}\mathbin{\circ}d\kappa\,\mathrm{d}\mathord{\lambda}
.

Alas, the term ‘tangential’ lift used in the literature is inherently flawed
even more than the terms ‘vertical’ and ‘horizontal’.
As terms like ‘tangential gradient’ might cause serious confusion,
we depart from our naming scheme as follows.

Definition 4.3

(spherical lift, gradient, divergence and Laplacian)
.

(i)

For all
\mathcal{X}\in\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\textstyle\Gamma^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}{\scriptstyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma^{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\right\rparen}

we call
\tanlift(\mathcal{X})\in\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\textstyle\Gamma^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}{\scriptstyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma^{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\right\rparen}
the
spherical lift of
\mathcal{X}

.

(ii)

The
spherical gradient
\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}

is defined by the relation

\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}f\in\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\textstyle\Gamma^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}{\scriptstyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma^{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}\left\lparen\tanlift(\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}})\right\rparen}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathord{\mathrm{s}\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}f\mathbin{,}\tanlift{\mathcal{X}}\right\rparen}=\tanlift(\mathcal{X})f

for
f\in\mathord{C^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\right\rparen}

and
\mathcal{X}\in\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\textstyle\Gamma^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}{\scriptstyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma^{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\right\rparen}
arbitrary.

(iii)

The
spherical divergence
\mathord{\mathrm{div}_{\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}

is defined via

\mathop{\mathscr{L}_{\tanlift\mathcal{X}}}\mathord{\mathrm{v}}|_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}=\mathord{\mathrm{div}_{\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}(\tanlift\mathcal{X})\mathbin{\cdot}\mathord{\mathrm{v}}|_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}

for
\mathcal{X}\in\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\textstyle\Gamma^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}{\scriptstyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma^{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\right\rparen}
arbitrary,
where
\mathop{\mathscr{L}}
again denotes the Lie derivative.

(iv)

The
spherical Laplace-Beltrami operator
\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}

is defined by

\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\mathord{\mathrm{div}_{\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}(\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}})
,
as usual.

∎

Basically, these are just the natural modifications of the elementary objects within our calculus.
E. g. it’s easily verified that

\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}f_{0}^{\mathrm{h}}=\tanlift(\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}f_{0}\mathbin{\circ}\mathord{\pi_{0|\mathrm{S}}})

holds for all
f_{0}\in\mathord{C^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\right\rparen}
.
Other statements from
Lemma
2.31
can be translated similarly.
In this paper we only consider the normalised surface measure on the sphere,
therefore weighted vertical gradients etc. do not appear.
We propose the notation

\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{v}}}}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}

for that we can use a boldfaced ‘v’ to signify the weighted vertical gradient
\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{v}}}}}
in case of a nonconstant fibre weight.

The algebraic side condition yields some more interesting effects.
We want to fix a very important consequence of the relation

(4.1)

\mathop{\Delta_{F}}\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{F}=-(\text{n}-1)\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{F}\qquad\text{for }F=\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}},

where the Laplacian is taken componentwise in standard Euclidean coordinates.
See
[
GS14
, Lemma 3.2]

and for a general proof on eigenvalues of the spherical Laplace-Beltrami
we refer e. g. to
[
DX13
, Theorem 1.4.5]
.

Lemma 4.4
.

It holds

\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}=-(\text{n}-1)\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}
,
where we also denote by
\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}
the spherical tensor Laplacian
and think of the vector field
\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}
as a
(1,0)
-
tensor field.

Proof.

For fixed
w\in\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
with
x\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\mathord{\pi_{0|\mathrm{S}}}(w)
we get that

\displaystyle\mathord{\mathrm{h}_{w}\left\lparen\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}|_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathbin{,}\horlift(w)\right\rparen}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}\mathord{\mathrm{h}_{w}\left\lparen\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}|_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathbin{,}\horlift(w)\right\rparen}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{x}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathbin{,}w\right\rparen}=\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{x}\left\lparen\mathop{\Delta_{F}}\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{F}\mathbin{,}w\right\rparen}.

Use
Equation (
4.1
)
.
∎

Consider the following Stratonovich SDE in
\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
:

(4.2)

\mathrm{d}\eta=\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\ \mathrm{d}t+\tanlift_{\eta}(-\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\Psi)\ \mathrm{d}t+\sigma\mathbin{\cdot}\tanlift_{\eta}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\text{n}}\frac{\partial}{\partial{x_{\eta}^{j}}}\right)\mathbin{\circ}\mathrm{d}W_{t},

where the chart
\left\lparen x_{\eta}^{1},x_{\eta}^{2},\ldots,x_{\eta}^{\text{n}}\right\rparen
at
\mathord{\pi_{0|\mathrm{S}}}(\eta)

provides normal coordinates
and
\sigma
is a nonnegative diffusion parameter.
Note that neither we rescale the potential
nor we incorporate a friction term.
We call
Equation (
4.2
)

the
fibre lay-down equation on
\mathord{\mathbb{M}}

or just
geometric fibre lay-down model
.
The corresponding Kolmogorov generator attains the form

(4.3)

L=\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}-\tanlift(\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\Psi)+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\,\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}.

We call
L
as in
Equation (
4.3
)

the
fibre lay-down generator
.
Per se this operator is defined for all smooth functions on the tangent space,
whilst the obvious choice for the domain of test functions is

D\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\right\rparen}
.
Modificating the proof of
Lemma
2.40
slightly
we get that

D_{0|\mathrm{S}}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\mathord{\pi_{0|\mathrm{S}}}^{\ast}\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\right\rparen}\mathbin{\otimes}\kappa^{\ast}\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\right\rparen}

is dense in
D
again.

In the next sections we restrict ourselves to computations substantially different from
Section 3
.
Briefly speaking, the differences occur due to the change of the fibre measure space
and affect some of the constants.

4.1.
Data conditions

Definition 4.5

(model Hilbert space
(D1)
)
.

Consider the probability space

(E,\mathfrak{E},\mu)=\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}},\mathop{\mathfrak{B}(\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}})},\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{s}}}}}\right\rparen,

where
\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{s}}}}}=\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}}\mathbin{\otimes_{\mathrm{loc}}}\nu

is the weighted Sasaki volume measure with

\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}
weighted by

\rho_{\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\exp(-\Psi)
such that

\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}}
is a probability measure on
(\mathord{\mathbb{M}},\mathop{\mathfrak{B}(\mathord{\mathbb{M}})})
,
and
\nu

is the normalised surface measure on

(F,\mathfrak{F})=\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}},\mathop{\mathfrak{B}(\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}})}\right\rparen
,
thus the fibre weight is a constant factor.
The model Hilbert space is

H\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen E;\mu\right\rparen}=\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{s}}}\right\rparen}
.
∎

Note that our choice of
\nu
is the only possible for a probability measure on the measurable fibre space
with a density that is invariant wrt. rotations.
Furthermore, we point out that up to slight modifications
we could keep the set of conditions
3.1
of
Section 3
.
The assumption of a weakly harmonic potential in
Lemma
3.4
,
which in the end was not necessary,
turns into another condition that can not be overcome so easily.
Specifically, we require the potential to satisfy the relation

(4.4)

\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\Psi^{\mathrm{v}}=(\text{n}-1)\,\Psi^{\mathrm{h}}\qquad\text{on }\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}

up to an additional constant summand.
This will become evident during the proof of the oncoming lemma.
Indeed, the fibre lay-down generator attains the form analogous to
[
GS14
, Equation 3.18]
under this assumption.
Later on in
Lemma
4.8

we seemingly get rid of
Assumption (
4.4
)

using the Poisson bracket again.
But it turns out that this result doesn’t fit our purposes
and we want to add
Assumption (
4.4
)

to the set of conditions on the potential.

Example 4.6

(
Assumption (
4.4
)
for Euclidean position space)
.

Let
\mathord{\mathbb{M}}=\mathbb{R}_{x}^{\text{n}}
be endowed with standard Euclidean metric

\mathord{\mathrm{m}}=\left\lparen\left\lparen\cdot\mathbin{,}\cdot\right\rparen_{\mathrm{euc}}\right\rparen_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{\text{n}}}
.
Then, the Riemannian semispray
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{euc}}
effectively is just the identity mapping.
The interested reader easily verifies this in local coordinates.
More formally,
every

a^{\prime}\in\mathord{\mathrm{T}^{\ast}\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\simeq\left\lparen\mathbb{R}_{x}^{\text{n}}\times\mathbb{R}_{v}^{\text{n}}\right\rparen^{\ast}

is identified with an
a=(a_{v},a_{x})^{\intercal}\in\mathbb{R}^{2\text{n}}
via

a^{\prime}(x,v)=\left\lparen v\mathbin{,}a_{v}\right\rparen_{\mathrm{euc}}+\left\lparen x\mathbin{,}a_{x}\right\rparen_{\mathrm{euc}}

for all
(x,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{x}^{\text{n}}\times\mathbb{R}_{v}^{\text{n}}
.
Then, the semispray
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{euc}}
is characterised by

\left\lparen\Omega^{\mathrm{h}}\mathbin{\circ}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{euc}}\right\rparen_{w}(a)=\left\lparen w\mathbin{,}a_{x}-a_{v}\right\rparen_{\mathrm{euc}}\qquad\text{for all }(x,w)\in\mathbb{R}_{x}^{\text{n}}\times\mathbb{R}_{v}^{\text{n}},a\in\mathbb{R}^{2\text{n}}

cf.
Remark
2.37
.
We have chosen the notation
a=(a_{v},a_{x})^{\intercal}
for sake of readability in view of this remark.
Hence, we can think of the mapping
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{euc}}(w)

as the gradient of
z\mapsto U_{w}(z)\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\left\lparen w\mathbin{,}z\right\rparen_{\mathrm{euc}}
.
When restricting the semispray to
\mathsf{Q}=\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathbb{R}_{x}^{\text{n}}}
,
i. e.
w\in\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}}
,
we calculate via usual integration by parts that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathbb{R}^{\text{n}}}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}f\ \mathrm{d}\nu=\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathbb{R}^{\text{n}}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}U_{v}(z)\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}f(v)\right\rparen_{\mathrm{euc}}\ \mathrm{d}\nu(v)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathbb{R}^{\text{n}}}}\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}U_{v}(z)\mathbin{\cdot}f(v)\ \mathrm{d}\nu(v)\overset{\eqref{eq:eigenvalue-eq}}{=}(\text{n}-1)\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathbb{R}^{\text{n}}}}\left\lparen v\mathbin{,}z\right\rparen_{\mathrm{euc}}\mathbin{\cdot}f(v)\ \mathrm{d}\nu(v)

for all
f\in\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathbb{R}^{\text{n}}_{x}}\right\rparen}
,
z\in\mathbb{R}^{\text{n}}
.
Cf.
[
GS14
, Lemma 3.3]
.

Now, let
f_{0}\in\mathord{C^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathbb{R}_{x}^{\text{n}}\right\rparen}

and
a=(a_{v},a_{x})^{\intercal}\in\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathsf{Q}}

with
d\pi_{0}(a)=a_{x}
as well as
d\kappa(a)=a_{v}=v\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\pi_{0}(a)
.
In this situation we have that

\mathord{\left\langle a\mathbin{,}df_{0}^{\mathrm{h}}\right\rangle}=\mathord{\left\langle(a_{v},a_{x})\mathbin{,}df_{0}\mathbin{\circ}d\kappa\right\rangle}=\mathord{\left\langle v\mathbin{,}df_{0}|_{\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathbb{R}^{\text{n}}}}\right\rangle}=\left\lparen v\mathbin{,}\frac{\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mux}}f_{0}}{\lvert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mux}}f_{0}\rvert}\mathbin{\circ}\mathord{\pi_{0|\mathrm{S}}}(v)\right\rparen_{\mathrm{euc}}.

Thus,

f_{0}^{\mathrm{h}}(z)=U_{\nicefrac{{\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mux}}f_{0}}}{{\lvert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mux}}f_{0}\rvert}}(x)}(z)

for all
z\in\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathbb{R}^{\text{n}}}
.
Both results can be combined as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathbb{R}^{\text{n}}}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{euc}}\Psi^{\mathrm{v}}\mathbin{\cdot}g_{0}^{\mathrm{h}}\ \mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathbb{R}^{\text{n}}}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{euc}}\left\lparen\Psi^{\mathrm{v}}\mathbin{\cdot}g_{0}^{\mathrm{h}}\right\rparen\ \mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle=(\text{n}-1)\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathbb{R}^{\text{n}}}}\left\lparen v\mathbin{,}\frac{\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathrm{euc}}}\Psi(x)}{\Psi(x)\,\lvert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathrm{euc}}}\Psi(x)\rvert}\right\rparen_{\mathrm{euc}}\mathbin{\cdot}\Psi^{\mathrm{v}}(v)\,g_{0}^{\mathrm{h}}(v)\ \mathrm{d}\nu(v)

\displaystyle=(\text{n}-1)\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathbb{R}^{\text{n}}}}\Psi^{\mathrm{h}}(v)\mathbin{\cdot}g_{0}^{\mathrm{h}}(v)\ \mathrm{d}\nu(v)

holds for all
g_{0}\in\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathbb{R}_{x}^{\text{n}}\right\rparen}

with the particular choice of

z=\frac{1}{\Psi(x)}\,\frac{\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathrm{euc}}}\Psi(x)}{\lvert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathrm{euc}}}\Psi(x)\rvert}
.
Note that we assume
\Psi>0
wlog. in view of
(P1)
.
Hence,
Assumption (
4.4
)
always is fulfilled.
∎

Lemma 4.7

(SAD-decomposition
(D3)
,
(D4)
,
(D6)
)
.

Let the potential
\Psi
be loc-Lipschitzian
such that
Assumption (
4.4
)
is fulfilled.
Consider the SAD
-
decomposition
L=S-A
on
D
with

\displaystyle Sf

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\,\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}f

\displaystyle\quad\text{and}\quad Af

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}f\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=-\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}f+\frac{1}{\text{n}-1}\,\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\Psi^{\mathrm{v}})

for all
f\in D
.

Then, the following assertions hold:

(i)

(S,D)
is symmetric and negative semidefinite.

(ii)

(A,D)
is antisymmetric.

(iii)

For all
f\in D
we have that

Lf\in\mathord{L^{1}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mu\right\rparen}
and

\int_{\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}Lf\ \mathrm{d}\mu=0
.

Proof.

(i)

Using integration by parts we see that

(S,D)
pregenerates the weighted spherical gradient form on
\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
.
Cf. the proof of part
(i)

of
Lemma
3.4
.

(ii)

The adjoint of

\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}\Psi^{\mathrm{h}}\overset{\eqref{eq:fld:potential-condition}}{=}\frac{1}{\text{n}-1}\,\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\Psi^{\mathrm{v}})

wrt.
\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{s}}}\right\rparen}
-
scalar product
is computed using
Lemma
4.4
as

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\displaystyle\left\lparen\frac{1}{\text{n}-1}\,\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\Psi^{\mathrm{v}})\right\rparen^{\ast}}{\textstyle\left\lparen\frac{1}{\text{n}-1}\,\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\Psi^{\mathrm{v}})\right\rparen^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\ast$}}}{\scriptstyle\left\lparen\frac{1}{\text{n}-1}\,\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\Psi^{\mathrm{v}})\right\rparen^{\ast}}{\scriptscriptstyle\left\lparen\frac{1}{\text{n}-1}\,\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\Psi^{\mathrm{v}})\right\rparen^{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\ast$}}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\text{n}-1}\,\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\Psi^{\mathrm{v}})-\frac{1}{\text{n}-1}\,\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\Psi^{\mathrm{v}})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\text{n}-1}\,\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\Psi^{\mathrm{v}})+\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\Psi^{\mathrm{v}}.

The rest follows as in the proof of part
(ii)

of
Lemma
3.4
.

(iii)

Follows with the parts
(i)

and
(ii)
.

∎

Giving up on the form of
(A,D)
as in
[
GS14
, Equation (3.22)]

and turning to a more intuitive one
in view of
Equation (
4.3
)
,
we gain weaker assumptions on the potential in general.

Lemma 4.8

(SAD-decomposition (2nd version))
.

If we define

\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}-\tanlift\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\Psi\right\rparen
,
then the assertions of
Lemma
4.7

are true without the
Assumption (
4.4
)
on
\Psi
.

Proof.

Indeed, we can copy the proof of
Proposition
3.7

and it’s enough that the configuration manifold
\mathsf{Q}
is a submanifold of
\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
.

First, we notice that both
\Omega
and
\mathbb{J}

restricted to
\mathord{\mathrm{T}^{\ast}\mathsf{Q}}
or
\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathsf{Q}}
respectively
are still a symplectic form and an almost complex structure respectively.
They are compatible with the restricted Sasaki metric,
thus generate the same Poisson bracket on
\mathsf{Q}
which in turn defines Hamiltonian vector fields

\mathsf{H}_{f}\in\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\textstyle\Gamma^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}{\scriptstyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma^{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathsf{Q}}\right\rparen}

for all
f\in\mathord{C^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathsf{Q}\right\rparen}
.
Second, we find that

\mathsf{H}_{f}(\rho)=-\rho\mathbin{\cdot}\left\lparen\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}f-\tanlift\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\Psi\right\rparen(f)\right\rparen

for all
f\in D=\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathsf{Q}\right\rparen}
,
by investigating the action of the Hamiltonian vector field
in local coordinates for
\mathsf{Q}
that respect the Ehresmann connection
and also provide a local trivialisation.
∎

In contrast to
Proposition
3.7

the statement of
Lemma
4.8

is not of much use for an application of the hypocoercivity method,
even though the operator

(A,D)=(-\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}},D)

there is the more natural formulation.
The simple reason is
that the two competing definitions of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}
not necessarily coincide on
D
.
But we need the operator as in
Lemma
4.7

during the characterisation of
(PA^{2}P,D)

specifically in
Equation (
4.8
)
.
However, this calculation is part of checking the hypocoercivity assumptions,
whereas condition
(D2)
,
the existence of a nice semigroup,
can be checked following the same steps as in
Section 3.1

for the antisymmetric operator of
Lemma
4.8
.

Moving on, the fibrewise average is defined the very same way as in
Section 3

just with ‘
\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
’ instead of ‘
\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{T}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
’.
Also, the form of the operator
(AP,D)

given in
Equation (
3.6
)

just changes marginally:

(4.5)

APf=-\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{\mathord{\pi_{0|\mathrm{S}}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\mathbin{\circ}\mathord{\pi_{0|\mathrm{S}}}\right\rparen}\qquad\text{for all }f\in D.

Indeed, the other statements concerning data conditions
translate to the fibre lay-down model on
\mathord{\mathbb{M}}
with minor modifications.
This is a little bit different
when it comes to the hypocoercivity conditions in the next section.
Nevertheless, we want to draw the readers attention to the fact
the reasoning for essential m-dissipativity of
(L,D)

under the assumption of loc-Lipschitzian potentials
does barely depend on the fibre measure space.
Arguments gleaned in
[
GS14
, Section 4]

on the notoriously subtile question of core property and
L
generating a semigroup stay valid.

Since the standard fibre is compact now,
we can simplify the proof
Lemma
3.8
a bit,
similar to
[
GS14
, Lemma 3.8]
.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let condition
(P1)
hold.
Then, we have

P(H)\subseteq\mathord{D\left\lparen S\right\rparen}
,
SP=0
,

P(D)\subseteq\mathord{D\left\lparen A\right\rparen}
and
AP(D)\subseteq\mathord{D\left\lparen A\right\rparen}
.
Furthermore,
1\in\mathord{D\left\lparen L\right\rparen}
and
L1=0
.

Proof.

The range
P(H)
is identified with a subset of
\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}\right\rparen}

via the vertical lift.
For any
f_{0}\in\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}\right\rparen}

there is an
\mathord{L^{2}}
-
approximating sequence
(f_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}}
in
\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\right\rparen}
.
Since the standard fibre
F=\mathord{\mathbb{S}^{\text{n}-1}}
is compact,
it holds
f_{n}^{\mathrm{v}}\in D
and
S(f_{n}^{\mathrm{v}})=0
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}
.
We conclude that

f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}\in\mathord{D\left\lparen S\right\rparen}
and
f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}\in\mathrm{Null}\left\lparen S\right\rparen

as
f_{n}^{\mathrm{v}}\longrightarrow f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}
in
H
as
n\to\infty
and

\mathord{\left\lparen S,\mathord{D\left\lparen S\right\rparen}\right\rparen}
is closed.

We fix an
f\in D
.
Choose
o\in\mathord{\mathbb{M}}
and
an open ball
\mathord{\mathbb{U}(o,r)}
centred at
o
with radius
r\in(0,\infty)
wrt. the intrinsic metric on
(\mathord{\mathbb{M}},\mathord{\mathrm{m}})
such that
the support of
f
is completely contained in

\mathord{\pi_{0|\mathrm{S}}}^{-1}(\mathord{\mathbb{U}(o,r)})\subseteq\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
.
Than, the support of
\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]
is contained in
\mathord{\mathbb{U}(o,r)}
.
Thus,
P_{S}f\in D
.
Therefore,
P(D)\subseteq D\subseteq\mathord{D\left\lparen A\right\rparen}
.

Besides, we calculate via chain rule that

(4.6)
