\displaystyle\begin{aligned} APf&=-\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}\left\lparen\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]^{\mathrm{v}}\right\rparen=-\mathord{\left\langle\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\mathbin{,}d\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\mathbin{\circ}d\pi_{0}\right\rangle}\\
&=-\mathord{\left\langle\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathbin{,}d\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\right\rangle}=-d\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f].\end{aligned}

Consequently,
AP(D)\subseteq D\subseteq\mathord{D\left\lparen A\right\rparen}
,
as the right-hand side of
Equation (
4.6
)

is smooth with compact support.
Let
\varphi\in\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}};[0,1]\right\rparen}
be a cut-off function
such that
\varphi=1
on
\mathord{\mathbb{U}(o,1)}
and
\varphi=0
on
\mathord{\mathbb{U}(o,2)}
.
Define
\varphi_{n}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\varphi(\nicefrac{{\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}}}{{n}})
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}
.
Note that

\lvert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\varphi_{n}(x)\rvert_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\leq\frac{1}{n}\,\lVert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\varphi\rVert_{\mathord{L^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{m}}\right\rparen}}
for all
x\in\mathord{\mathbb{M}}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}
.
By construction, we have that

AP\varphi_{n}^{\mathrm{v}}=-\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\varphi_{n}^{\mathrm{v}}=\mathord{\left\langle\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\mathbin{,}d\varphi_{n}\mathbin{\circ}d\pi_{0}\right\rangle}=-d\varphi_{n}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{as }n\to\infty

pointwise and in
\mathord{L^{2}}
-
sense.
Since
\mathord{\left\lparen A,\mathord{D\left\lparen A\right\rparen}\right\rparen}
is closed,
we have
1\in\mathord{D\left\lparen A\right\rparen}
and
A1=0
.

Since we know form
Lemma
4.7
part
(i)

that
S\varphi_{n}^{\mathrm{v}}=0
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
we have
L\varphi_{n}^{\mathrm{v}}=-A\varphi_{n}^{\mathrm{v}}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
The sequence
\left\lparen L\varphi_{n}^{\mathrm{v}}\right\rparen_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

converges in
H
to 0 as
n\to\infty
.
∎

4.2.
Hypocoercivity conditions

Lemma 4.10

(algebraic relation
(H1)
)
.

Let
\Psi
be loc-Lipschitzian such that

\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}}=\exp(-\Psi)\,\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}

is a probability measure on
(\mathord{\mathbb{M}},\mathop{\mathfrak{B}(\mathord{\mathbb{M}})})
.
Then, we have
PAP|_{D}=0
.
Cf.
[
GS14
, Proposition 3.11]
.

Proof.

Using only
[
GS14
, Lemma 3.1]

and
Equation (
4.5
)

we calculate that

\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}APf\ \mathrm{d}\nu\overset{\eqref{eq:fld:APf-Eq2}}{=}\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}-\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{x}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f](x)\right\rparen}\ \mathrm{d}\nu=0

holds for all
f\in D
and
x\in\mathord{\mathbb{M}}
.
The rest of the proof works as in
Lemma
3.18
.
∎

Lemma 4.11

(microscopic coercivity
(H2)
)
.

Let
\Psi
loc-Lipschitzian such that

\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}}=\exp(-\Psi)\,\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}

is a probability measure on
(\mathord{\mathbb{M}},\mathop{\mathfrak{B}(\mathord{\mathbb{M}})})
.
Then, condition
(H2)
holds with

\Lambda_{m}=(\text{n}-1)\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}
.
Cf.
[
GS14
, Proposition 3.12]
.

Proof.

The proof works the same way as in
Lemma
3.19

using the Poincaré inequality for the spherical measure, see
[
Bec89
, Theorem 2]
.
∎

For proving condition
(H3)
, we want to characterise the operator
(PA^{2}P,D)

as a weighted horizontal Laplace-Beltrami composed with fibrewise average again.
Then, we get essential m-dissipativity of this operator
as in
Corollary
3.20
.
Mirroring the computations in
Equation (
3.8
)

we calculate that

(4.7)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}-\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\left\lparen-\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{\mathord{\pi_{0|\mathrm{S}}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}\left[f\right]\mathbin{\circ}\mathord{\pi_{0|\mathrm{S}}}\right\rparen}\right\rparen\ \mathrm{d}\nu\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:fld:APf-Eq2}}{=}&\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{x}\left\lparen v\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}\left[f\right](x)\right\rparen}\ \mathrm{d}\nu(v)+\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{x}\left\lparen v\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{v}^{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\right\rparen(x)\right\rparen}\ \mathrm{d}\nu(v)\\
={}&\frac{1}{\text{n}}\,\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f](x)\end{aligned}

for all
v\in\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
with
x\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\mathord{\pi_{0|\mathrm{S}}}(v)
.

As for
Equation (
3.9
)

we use the proof of part
(ii)
of
Lemma
4.7

to get that

(4.8)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &P_{S}\left\lparen\frac{1}{\text{n}-1}\,\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\Psi^{\mathrm{v}})(APf)\right\rparen(v)=\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\Psi^{\mathrm{v}}\mathbin{\cdot}APf\ \mathrm{d}\nu\\
={}&-\frac{1}{\text{n}}\,\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{x}\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\Psi(x)\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f](x)\right\rparen}=-\frac{1}{\text{n}}\,\mathord{\nabla_{\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\Psi}}(\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f])(x)\end{aligned}

for all
v\in\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
with
x\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\mathord{\pi_{0|\mathrm{S}}}(v)
.

Together
Equation (
3.8
)

and
Equation (
3.9
)

imply the relation

(4.9)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} PA^{2}Pf&=P_{S}A^{2}Pf=\frac{1}{\text{n}}\mathbin{\cdot}\left\lparen\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\mathbin{\circ}\mathord{\pi_{0|\mathrm{S}}}-\mathord{\nabla_{\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\Psi}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\mathbin{\circ}\mathord{\pi_{0|\mathrm{S}}}\right\rparen\\
&=\frac{1}{\text{n}}\mathbin{\cdot}\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}}}}\left\lparen\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]^{\mathrm{v}}\right\rparen=\frac{1}{\text{n}}\mathbin{\cdot}\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}}}}(P_{S}f)\end{aligned}

for all
f\in D
.
Compare this to
[
GS14
, Equation (3.27)]
.

Proposition 4.12

(macroscopic coercivity
(H3)
)
.

Let
\Psi
loc-Lipschitzian such that

\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}}=\exp(-\Psi)\,\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}

is a probability measure on
(\mathord{\mathbb{M}},\mathop{\mathfrak{B}(\mathord{\mathbb{M}})})

satisfying the
Poincaré inequality (
3.3
)
.
Then, condition
(H3)
is fulfilled
with
\Lambda_{M}=\frac{1}{\text{n}}\,\Lambda
.
Cf.
[
GS14
, Proposition 3.14]
.

Proof.

As before, we compute that for all
f\in D
holds

\displaystyle\lVert APf\rVert_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}(APf)^{2}\bigr|_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\ \mathrm{d}\nu\,\mathrm{d}\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}}(x)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:fld:APf-Eq2}}{=}\int_{\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\int_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\textstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}{\scriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{S}_{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}}}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{x}\left\lparen v\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f](x)\right\rparen}^{2}\quad\mathrm{d}\nu(v)\,\mathrm{d}\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\text{n}}\int_{\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\lvert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f](x)\rvert_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}^{2}\quad\mathrm{d}\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}}(x)=\frac{1}{\text{n}}\,\lVert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\to\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}\right\rparen}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\text{n}}\,\Lambda\lVert\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]-\left\lparen\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\mathbin{,}1\right\rparen_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}\right\rparen}}\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}\right\rparen}}^{2}

using the Poincaré inequality of the weighted base measure.
The claim follows with
[
GS14
, Corollary 2.13]
,
since
(PA^{2}P,D)
is essentially m-dissipative
due to the modification of
Corollary
3.20

to the case of
\mathsf{Q}=\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
.
∎

This time, we do not even need
\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}
to be a spray
when checking the first part of condition
(H4)
.
Compare the following Lemmas
4.13
and
4.14

as well as their proofs
to
[
GS14
, Proposition 3.15]
.

Lemma 4.13

(boundedness of
(BS,D)
, first part of
(H4)
)
.

Let
\Psi
be loc-Lipschitzian such that

\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}}=\exp(-\Phi)\,\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}

is a probability measure on
(\mathord{\mathbb{M}},\mathop{\mathfrak{B}(\mathord{\mathbb{M}})})
.
Then, with
c_{1}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=(\text{n}-1)\frac{\sigma^{2}}{4}
it holds that

\lVert BSf\rVert_{H}\leq c_{1}\lVert(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}-P_{j})f\rVert_{H}\qquad\text{for all }f\in D

and
P_{j}\in\{P,P_{S}\}
,
j\in\{1,2\}
.

Proof.

Let
f\in D
be fixed.
Then, we observe that

\displaystyle SAPf

\displaystyle=SAP_{S}f=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\,\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\mathbb{S}}}}(-\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}(P_{S}f))

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\,(\text{n}-1)\mathbin{\cdot}\mathcal{H}_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}(P_{S}f)=-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\,(\text{n}-1)APf

by
Lemma
4.4
,
and since

\left\lparen Ah\mathbin{,}1\right\rparen_{H}=0
holds for all
h\in D

by
Lemma
4.7
part
(ii)
.
∎

Lemma 4.14

(boundedness of
(BA(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}-P),D)
, second part of
(H4)
))
.

Let all the conditions of
Theorem
4.1

on the potential hold.
Then, there exists a constant
c_{2}\in(0,\infty)
such that

\lVert BA(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}-P)f\rVert_{H}\leq c_{2}\lVert(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}-P_{j})f\rVert_{H}\qquad\text{for all }f\in D

and
P_{j}\in\{P,P_{S}\}
,
j\in\{1,2\}
.

Proof.

Let
f\in D
and
g\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}-PA^{2}P)f

as in the proof of
Lemma
3.23
.
Now, the relevant estimate reads as

\lVert\mathchoice{\displaystyle(BA)^{\ast}}{\textstyle(BA)^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\ast$}}}{\scriptstyle(BA)^{\ast}}{\scriptscriptstyle(BA)^{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\ast$}}}g\rVert_{H}\leq\lVert\lvert\operatorname{Hess}_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}\left\lparen\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\right\rparen\rvert^{\mathrm{v}}\rVert_{H}+\frac{1}{\text{n}}\lVert\lvert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\Phi\rvert_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}^{\mathrm{v}}\mathbin{\cdot}\lvert\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\operatorname{E}_{\nu}[f]\rvert_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}^{\mathrm{v}}\rVert_{H}.

In view of
Equation (
4.9
)

we have the solution
u\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=Pf
of the elliptic equation

\displaystyle u-\frac{1}{\text{n}}\,\mathop{\Delta_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{h}}}}}P_{S}u=g

in

\displaystyle\left\{u\in\mathord{\pi_{0|\mathrm{S}}}^{\ast}\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}};\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}\right\rparen}\ \middle|\ \exists\,f_{0}\in\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\right\rparen}\colon\ u=f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}-\left\lparen f_{0}^{\mathrm{v}}\mathbin{,}1\right\rparen_{H}\right\}.

The proof is completed as in
Lemma
3.23
.
∎

Combining the results of
Section 4.1
and
Section 4.2

our main theorem,

Theorem
4.1
,
follows from the
2.2
.

5.
Existence of martingale solutions and
L^{2}
-exponential ergodicity

Finally, we show existence of
L
-
martingale solutions to the SDEs investigated in this article.
The strong mixing of the corresponding semigroups with exponential rate of convergence then implies
their
\mathord{L^{2}}
-
exponential ergodicity.
Let the configuration manifold

\mathsf{Q}\in\{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}},\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\}

be the state space
E=\mathsf{Q}

as mentioned in the end of
Section 1
.
For basic notions used in the following theorem
we refer to
[
Sta99
]
,
[
Tru00
]
and
[
Tru03
]
.

Theorem 5.1

(existence of martingale solutions)
.

If
\mathsf{Q}=\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
,
let the assumptions of
Theorem
3.2
hold.
If
\mathsf{Q}=\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
,
let the assumptions of
Theorem
4.1
hold.
Then, there is a Hunt process

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{HP}}=\left\lparen\Omega,\mathfrak{A},\mathord{\mathfrak{F}}=\left\lparen\mathord{\mathfrak{F}_{t}}\right\rparen_{t\in[0,\infty)},\eta=\left\lparen\eta_{t}\right\rparen_{t\in[0,\infty)},\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{P}}_{v}\right\rparen_{v\in\mathsf{Q}}\right\rparen

properly associated in the resolvent sense with
\mathord{\left\lparen L,\mathord{D\left\lparen L\right\rparen}\right\rparen}

having infinite life-time and continuous paths
\mathord{\mathbb{P}}_{v}
-
almost surely for all
v\in\mathsf{Q}
.
I. e. if
(G_{a})_{a\in(0,\infty)}
denotes the resolvent
corresponding to
\mathord{\left\lparen L,\mathord{D\left\lparen L\right\rparen}\right\rparen}
,
then the transition resolvent
(R_{a})_{a\in(0,\infty)}
yields
a quasi-continuous version
R_{a}f
of
G_{a}f

for all
f\in\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathsf{Q};\mu\right\rparen}
and
a\in(0,\infty)
,
where
R_{a}f(v)=\int_{(0,\infty)}\exp(-as)\operatorname{E}_{v}[f(\eta_{t})]\,\mathord{\lambda}(\mathrm{d}s)
.

Moreover, for quasi every initial point
v\in\mathsf{Q}

the probability measure
\mathord{\mathbb{P}}_{v}
solves the martingale problem for

\left\lparen L,C^{2}_{c}(\mathsf{Q})\right\rparen
, i.e.
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{HP}}
is a martingale solution to
either (
3.1
) if
\mathsf{Q}=\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}

or to (
4.2
) if
\mathsf{Q}=\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}

for quasi every initial point
v\in\mathsf{Q}
.

Proof.

As we have seen before,

D=\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathsf{Q}\right\rparen}
is a core of
\mathord{\left\lparen L,\mathord{D\left\lparen L\right\rparen}\right\rparen}
,
see
Theorem
3.17

and the explanations on page
4.1
.
Observe that
D
also is an algebra which separates the points of
\mathsf{Q}
.
Thus,
\mathord{\left\lparen L,\mathord{D\left\lparen L\right\rparen}\right\rparen}
defines a generalised Dirichlet form fulfilling the assumptions of
[
Sta99
, Theorem IV.2.2]
.
This theorem provides a special standard process
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{HP}}

properly associated with
\mathord{\left\lparen L,\mathord{D\left\lparen L\right\rparen}\right\rparen}
in the resolvent sense.
Now, infinite life-time follows from
(D7)
,
i. e. conservativity,
together with
[
Sta99
, Theorem IV.3.8 (ii)]
.
Moreover, continuous paths are obtained via
[
Tru03
, Theorem 3.3]
.
Summarising,
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{HP}}
is a Hunt process indeed.
For the statement concerning the martingale problem
see
[
CG08
, Corollary 1]
and its proof.
Note that there are even some finer statements on the martingale problem,
cf.
[
Tru00
]
.
∎

Now, we turn to the matter of ergodicity.
Consider the probability measure
\mathord{\mathbb{P}}
on
(\Omega,\mathfrak{A})
be given as

\mathord{\mathbb{P}}(A)\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\int_{\mathsf{Q}}\mathord{\mathbb{P}}_{v}(A)\ \mu(\mathrm{d}v)\qquad\text{for all }A\in\mathfrak{A}.

We estimate for all
g\in\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathsf{Q};\mu\right\rparen}
with
\operatorname{E}_{\mu}[g]=0
and
t\in(0,\infty)
that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{t}\int_{[0,t)}g(\eta_{s})\,\mathord{\lambda}(\mathrm{d}s)\right\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{P}}\right\rparen}}^{2}=\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{t^{2}}\int_{[0,t)^{2}}g(\eta_{s})g(\eta_{u})\ \mathord{\lambda}(\mathrm{d}(s,u))\ \mathrm{d}\mathord{\mathbb{P}}

(5.1)

\displaystyle=\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{t^{2}}\int_{[0,t)^{2}}\operatorname{E}_{\mathord{\mathbb{P}}}[g(\eta_{s})g(\eta_{u})]\,\mathord{\lambda}(\mathrm{d}(s,u))=\frac{2}{t^{2}}\int_{[0,t)}\int_{[0,s)}\operatorname{E}_{\mathord{\mathbb{P}}}[g(\eta_{s})g(\eta_{u})]\,\mathord{\lambda}(\mathrm{d}u)\,\mathord{\lambda}(\mathrm{d}s)\end{aligned}

(5.2)

\displaystyle=\begin{aligned} \frac{2}{t^{2}}\int_{[0,t)}\int_{[0,s)}\left\lparen T_{s-u}g\mathbin{,}g\right\rparen_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mu\right\rparen}}\,\mathord{\lambda}(\mathrm{d}u)\,\mathord{\lambda}(\mathrm{d}s)\end{aligned}

(5.3)

\displaystyle=\begin{aligned} \frac{4}{t^{2}}\int_{[0,2t)}\int_{[0,t)}\left\lparen T_{v}g\mathbin{,}g\right\rparen_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mu\right\rparen}}\,\mathord{\lambda}(\mathrm{d}v)\,\mathord{\lambda}(\mathrm{d}w)=\frac{8}{t}\int_{[0,t)}\left\lparen T_{v}g\mathbin{,}g\right\rparen_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mu\right\rparen}}\,\mathord{\lambda}(\mathrm{d}v)\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{t}\,\lVert g\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mu\right\rparen}}\int_{[0,t)}\lVert T_{v}g\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mu\right\rparen}}\,\mathord{\lambda}(\mathrm{d}v).

At step (
5.1
) we use Fubini.
Afterwards at step (
5.2
),
we can ensure
u<s
for symmetry reasons
and transform expectation wrt.
\mathord{\mathbb{P}}
using the (weak) Markov property.
Then, at step (
5.3
)
we apply the 2D-transformation formula with
v=s-u
and
w=s+u
,
and finish with the Cauchy-Bunyakovsky-Schwarz inequality.

The previous estimate shows that
using our main theorems –
depending on
\mathsf{Q}
either
Theorem
3.2

or
Theorem
4.1
–
we not only can infer convergence to 0 as
t\to\infty
,
but also the rate of convergence is explicitly computable.
Indeed, with
g
as before we gain

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{t}\int_{[0,t)}g(\eta_{s})\,\mathord{\lambda}(\mathrm{d}s)\right\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{P}}\right\rparen}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{t}\,\lVert g\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mu\right\rparen}}\int_{[0,t)}\lVert T_{v}g\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mu\right\rparen}}\,\mathord{\lambda}(\mathrm{d}v)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{t}\,\lVert g\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mu\right\rparen}}^{2}\int_{[0,t)}\kappa_{1}\mathord{\mathrm{e}}^{-v\kappa_{2}}\,\mathord{\lambda}(\mathrm{d}v)

\displaystyle=\frac{8}{t}\mathbin{\cdot}\frac{\kappa_{1}}{\kappa_{2}}\,\left\lparen 1-\mathord{\mathrm{e}}^{-t\kappa_{2}}\right\rparen\mathbin{\cdot}\lVert g\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mu\right\rparen}}^{2}.

Thus, we proved the following corollary
after reducing everything to zero-mean functions wrt.
\mu
.

Corollary 5.2

(
\mathord{L^{2}}
-
exponentially ergodicity with optimal rate and explicit constants)
.

If
\mathsf{Q}=\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
,
let the assumptions of
Theorem
3.2
hold.
If
\mathsf{Q}=\mathord{\mathrm{S}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
,
let the assumptions of
Theorem
4.1
hold.
Moreover, let
\kappa_{1}
and
\kappa_{2}
be the constants form these respective theorems.
Then, we have

\left\lVert\frac{1}{t}\int_{[0,t)}f(\eta_{s})\,\mathord{\lambda}(\mathrm{d}s)-\operatorname{E}_{\mu}[f]\right\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{P}}\right\rparen}}\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{t}}\mathbin{\cdot}\sqrt{\frac{2\kappa_{1}}{\kappa_{2}}\,\left\lparen 1-\mathord{\mathrm{e}}^{-t\kappa_{2}}\right\rparen}\mathbin{\cdot}\lVert f-\operatorname{E}_{\mu}[f]\rVert_{\mathord{L^{2}\left\lparen\mu\right\rparen}}

for all
t\in(0,\infty)
.

Remark 5.3
.

In the title of
Corollary
5.2

we claim that the rate
t^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
is optimal.
This is obvious in the case that
the spectrum of the generator
\mathord{\left\lparen L,\mathord{D\left\lparen L\right\rparen}\right\rparen}
has,
apart from the eigenvalue zero,
the largest element
-\kappa<0

which is an eigenvalue of
\mathord{\left\lparen L,\mathord{D\left\lparen L\right\rparen}\right\rparen}
.
Evidently, all the inequality in the estimates prior to
Corollary
5.2

are equalities when choosing the function
g
there as the eigenvector corresponding to
-\kappa
.
Hence, the rate of convergence in
Corollary
5.2

is sharp with
\kappa_{1}=1
and
\kappa_{2}=\kappa
.

In situations where
\mathord{\left\lparen L,\mathord{D\left\lparen L\right\rparen}\right\rparen}
can be controlled by a Lyapunov function,
see e. g.
[
HM19
]
in case of purely Euclidean setting,
one obtains also exponential rates of convergence for the corresponding semigroups;
even in (weighted) total variation distance.
This implies pointwise convergence of the semigroup applied to test functions at an exponential rate.
But even this convergence with an exponential rate would not give a better rate as the one in
Corollary
5.2
.

As in
[
CG10
]
,
we call the martingale solutions to the SDEs investigated in this article
L^{2}
-
exponential ergodic
,
i. e. ergodic with a rate that corresponds to exponential convergence of the corresponding semigroups.
∎

Appendix A
Some expressions in local coordinates

A chart
x=\left\lparen x^{j}\right\rparen_{j=1}^{\text{n}}
with domain
U\subseteq\mathord{\mathbb{M}}

induces local coordinates

\left\lparen v^{k}\right\rparen_{k=1}^{2\text{n}}
for the preimage
V\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\pi_{0}^{-1}(U)
in a natural way:

v^{j}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=x^{j}\mathbin{\circ}\pi_{0}\quad\text{and}\quad v^{\text{n}+j}\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}\hss}\raisebox{-1.29167pt}{$\cdot$}}=\left\lparen dx^{j}\right\rparen^{\sharp}\qquad\text{for }j\in\{1,\ldots,\text{n}\}.

We might write
\pi_{0}^{\ast}x=x\mathbin{\circ}\pi_{0}=(v^{j})_{j=1}^{\text{n}}
and

dx=(v^{\text{n}+i})_{j=1}^{\text{n}}
,
where the latter shorthand doesn’t lead to confusion as
we denote the Riemannian volume form by
\mathrm{d}\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}
.

From
[
GK02
, Lemma 4.1]

we know some particular vertical and horizontal lifts:

\vertlift\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{i}}\right)=\frac{\partial}{\partial v^{\text{n}+i}}\quad\text{and}\quad\horlift\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{i}}\right)=\frac{\partial}{\partial v^{i}}-\sum_{j,\ell\in\{1,\ldots,\text{n}\}}\left(\Gamma^{\ell}_{ij}\mathbin{\circ}\pi_{0}\right)v^{\text{n}+j}\frac{\partial}{\partial v^{\text{n}+\ell}}

for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,\text{n}\}
.
Next, the following expression of semisprays has been taken
from
[
Buc06
, Section 1]
:
A semispray
\mathcal{H}
reads in local coordinate form as

\mathcal{H}=\sum_{j=1}^{\text{n}}v^{\text{n}+j}\partial v^{j}-2G^{j}(\pi_{0}^{\ast}x,dx)\,\partial v^{\text{n}+j}.

The family
\left\lparen G^{j}\right\rparen_{j=1}^{\text{n}}
of functions on
V

is characterised by functions

N^{i}_{j}=\frac{\partial G^{i}}{\partial v^{\text{n}+j}}
,
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,\text{n}\}
,
which are given pointwisely by

\left.\horlift\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{i}}\right)\right|_{u}=\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial v^{i}}\right|_{u}-\sum_{j=1}^{\text{n}}N^{j}_{i}(u)\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial v^{\text{n}+j}}\right|_{u}

for
u\in\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
arbitrary.
For instance,

[
Buc06
, Equation (19)]

yields coefficients
(G^{j})
of a semispray
corresponding to a given Lagrangian
\mathsf{L}
.
Recall
Example
2.36
.

In the following lemmas,
we prove some formulae used in
Section 3
.

Lemma A.1
.

For all
k\in\{1,\ldots,\text{n}\}
holds that

\mathord{\left[\mathcal{H}\mathbin{,}\vertlift\left\lparen\partial x^{k}\right\rparen\right]}=\horlift\left\lparen\partial x^{k}\right\rparen-\sum_{j=1}^{\text{n}}N_{k}^{j}\mathbin{\cdot}\vertlift\left\lparen\partial x^{j}\right\rparen.

Proof.

Let
k\in\{1,\ldots,\text{n}\}
.
Then, we calculate that

\displaystyle\mathord{\left[\mathcal{H}\mathbin{,}\vertlift\left\lparen\partial x^{k}\right\rparen\right]}

\displaystyle=\mathord{\left[\mathcal{H}\mathbin{,}\partial v^{\text{n}+k}\right]}=\sum_{j=1}^{\text{n}}\mathord{\left[v^{\text{n}+j}\partial v^{j}\mathbin{,}\partial v^{\text{n}+k}\right]}-2\mathord{\left[G^{j}\partial v^{\text{n}+j}\mathbin{,}\partial v^{\text{n}+k}\right]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{\text{n}}\,{\underbrace{\frac{\partial v^{\text{n}+j}}{\partial v^{\text{n}+k}}}_{=\delta_{jk}}}\mathbin{\cdot}\partial v^{j}+v^{\text{n}+j}\underbrace{\mathord{\left[\partial v^{j}\mathbin{,}\partial v^{\text{n}+k}\right]}}_{=0}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-2\biggl(\frac{\partial G^{j}}{\partial v^{\text{n}+k}}\mathbin{\cdot}\partial v^{\text{n}+j}+G_{j}\underbrace{\mathord{\left[\partial v^{\text{n}+j}\mathbin{,}\partial v^{\text{n}+k}\right]}}_{=0}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{\text{n}}\delta_{jk}\mathbin{\cdot}\partial v^{j}-2N_{k}^{j}\mathbin{\cdot}\partial v^{\text{n}+j}=\partial v^{k}-2\sum_{j=1}^{\text{n}}N_{k}^{j}\mathbin{\cdot}\partial v^{\text{n}+j}

\displaystyle=\horlift\left\lparen\partial x^{k}\right\rparen-\sum_{j=1}^{\text{n}}N_{k}^{j}\mathbin{\cdot}\partial v^{\text{n}+j}=\horlift\left\lparen\partial x^{k}\right\rparen-\sum_{j=1}^{\text{n}}N_{k}^{j}\mathbin{\cdot}\vertlift\left\lparen\partial x^{j}\right\rparen.

∎

The next formula appears to be rather intuitive:

Lemma A.2
.

For all
\mathcal{X}\in\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\textstyle\Gamma^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}{\scriptstyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma^{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\right\rparen}
holds that

\mathord{\mathrm{h}\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathord{\mathrm{s}}}}\horlift(\mathcal{X})\mathbin{,}\mathcal{H}\right\rparen}=\mathord{\mathrm{m}_{\pi_{0}}\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}}^{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}(\mathcal{X}\mathbin{\circ}\pi_{0})\mathbin{,}\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\right\rparen}.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{X}\in\mathord{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\textstyle\Gamma^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}{\scriptstyle\Gamma^{\infty}}{\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma^{\raise-0.21529pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\infty$}}}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}\right\rparen}
.
Then, the Koszul formula
describing the Levi-Civita connection
\mathord{\nabla^{\mathord{\mathrm{s}}}}
wrt.
\mathord{\mathrm{s}}
uniquely
reads as in our special instance as

\displaystyle 2\,\mathord{\mathrm{h}\left\lparen\mathord{\nabla_{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathord{\mathrm{s}}}}\horlift(\mathcal{X})\mathbin{,}\mathcal{H}\right\rparen}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{h}\left\lparen\horlift(\mathcal{X})\mathbin{,}\mathcal{H}\right\rparen}\right\rparen+\horlift(\mathcal{X})\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{h}\left\lparen\mathcal{H}\mathbin{,}\mathcal{H}\right\rparen}\right\rparen

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathcal{H}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathrm{h}\left\lparen\mathcal{H}\mathbin{,}\horlift(\mathcal{X})\right\rparen}\right\rparen-\mathord{\mathrm{h}\left\lparen\horlift(\mathcal{X})\mathbin{,}\mathord{\left[\mathcal{H}\mathbin{,}\mathcal{H}\right]}\right\rparen}-\mathord{\mathrm{h}\left\lparen\mathcal{H}\mathbin{,}\mathord{\left[\horlift(\mathcal{X})\mathbin{,}\mathcal{H}\right]}\right\rparen}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathord{\mathrm{h}\left\lparen\mathcal{H}\mathbin{,}\mathord{\left[\mathcal{H}\mathbin{,}\horlift(\mathcal{X})\right]}\right\rparen}

\displaystyle=\horlift(\mathcal{X})\left\lparen|\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}|_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}^{2}\right\rparen+2\mathord{\mathrm{h}\left\lparen\mathcal{H}\mathbin{,}\mathord{\left[\mathcal{H}\mathbin{,}\horlift(\mathcal{X})\right]}\right\rparen}

\displaystyle=\mathord{\mathrm{h}\left\lparen\horlift(\mathcal{X})\mathbin{,}\mathord{\nabla_{\mkern-3.0mu\mathord{\mathrm{h}}}}\left\lparen|\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}|_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}^{2}\right\rparen\right\rparen}+2\mathord{\mathrm{h}\left\lparen\mathcal{H}\mathbin{,}\mathord{\left[\mathcal{H}\mathbin{,}\horlift(\mathcal{X})\right]}\right\rparen}.

First, we note that

d\pi_{0}\mathord{\left[\mathcal{H}\mathbin{,}\horlift(\mathcal{X})\right]}=\mathord{\left[d\pi_{0}\mathcal{H}\mathbin{,}d\pi_{0}\horlift(\mathcal{X})\right]}=\mathord{\left[\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}\mathbin{,}\mathcal{X}\mathbin{\circ}\pi_{0}\right]}=\mathord{\nabla_{\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}}^{\mathord{\mathrm{s}}}}{(\mathcal{X}\mathbin{\circ}\pi_{0})}.

Second, the value of
|\mathord{\mathrm{Id}}_{\mathord{\mathrm{T}\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}}|_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}^{2}

does not specifically depend on the current position
and therefore it could be approximated just by functions from
\kappa^{\ast}\mathord{C_{c}^{\infty}\left\lparen\mathord{\mathbb{M}}\right\rparen}
.
In other words, this function is a horizontal lift
and the horizontal gradient of a horizontal lift equals 0 always.
Hence, the claim is proven.
∎

Appendix B
Miscellaneous

The following lemma is pretty elementary and rather an intuitive statement on weighted metric spaces.
Obviously, its assumptions are fulfilled by the exponential type weight as chosen in this article.

Lemma B.1
.

Let some given base weight
\rho_{\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}
strictly positive and loc
-
Lipschitzian.
Denote by
d_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}
and
d_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}
the metrics wrt. the nonweighted and weighted Riemannian metric respectively.
Then, those metrics induce equivalent topologies.
In particular, by
(M2)

the weighted manifold
(\mathord{\mathbb{M}},\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}})
is complete as a metric space.

Proof.

Let
(x_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a
d_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}
-
Cauchy sequence.
Since
\mathord{\mathbb{M}}
is finite dimensional,
this sequence is contained in a compactum
K\subseteq\mathord{\mathbb{M}}
.
On
K
the weight function is continuous,
thus it attains minimum and maximum.
Let
\varepsilon\in(0,\infty)
.
Then, we have for some
N_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}
and all
n_{1},n_{2}\geq N_{\varepsilon}
that

\frac{\varepsilon}{2}>d_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}(x_{n_{1}},x_{n_{2}})\geq\inf_{y\in K}\rho_{\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}(y)^{2}\mathbin{\cdot}d_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}(x_{n_{1}},x_{n_{2}}).

Hence,
(x_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a
d_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}
-
Cauchy sequence
and by
(M2)
it converges to
x\in\mathord{\mathbb{M}}
.
This
d_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}
-
limit also is the
d_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}
-
limit:

d_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}(x_{n_{1}},x)\leq d_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}(x_{n_{1}},x_{n_{2}})+\lVert\rho_{\mathord{\mathbb{M}}}^{2}\rVert_{\mathord{L^{\infty}\left\lparen K;\mathord{\lambda_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}}\right\rparen}}\mathbin{\cdot}d_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}(x_{n_{2}},x)<2\mathbin{\cdot}\frac{\varepsilon}{2}=\varepsilon

for
n_{2}\geq N_{\varepsilon}
large enough.

If we start with a
d_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}
-
Cauchy sequence,
then it is a
d_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}
-
Cauchy sequence
by a similar estimate.
If the
d_{\mathord{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{m}}}}
-
limit exists,
it’s easily verified that it also is the
d_{\mathord{\mathrm{m}}}
-
limit.
∎
